# Death Leak FPS and performance work — 2026-10-23

Status: implemented, measured on physical NVE7 GPU, and pushed; no release-complete claim. Scope: debug-mode FPS counter and active-gameplay renderer optimization.

Outcome: exact-staged-source GPU run improved active Forest frame p50 21.9874 -> 20.9234 ms and p95 23.7888 -> 22.7963 ms at 1280x720 with FPS visible. Unrelated worktree changes preserved. No release acceptance claimed.

## Baseline preflight — 2026-10-23
- Branch `feature/linux-renderer-foundation`; `main.cpp` clean at HEAD, existing opt-in `--show-fps` is visible in the gameplay HUD but no explicit debug-mode switch exists. `--profile` only writes terminal timing; likely interpretation is make debug/profile mode imply the on-screen FPS counter.
- Worktree has unrelated dirty `SDLRenderer.cpp`, integration test and status changes plus many untracked worker files. Do not stage these. Physical Xorg `:0` and `/dev/dri` are present; strict accelerated GPU captures have precedent on nouveau NVE7. Building unique baseline executable now.

## Physical GPU baseline and diagnosis
- Unique pre-change binary `port/build/performance-baseline/deathleak-linux`, 1280x720 Xorg `:0`, SDL OpenGL accelerated=1/software=0/vsync=1, nouveau NVE7. Active Forest-1 scripted movement/fire route `B:360`, fixed simulation dt 1/60, 360 frames, warmup 120, `--profile`, lossless endpoint capture. Log: `port/build/performance-baseline/baseline.log`; endpoint health 25, 7 orbs, `(2808.99,3118.11)`.
- Measured frame p50 39.726 ms, p95 45.154 ms (~25.2/22.1 FPS), all 240 profiled frames >33.3 ms. Update total 0.151 s, draw/present 15.316 s; renderer drew 31,461 sprite calls, copy 5.033 s, present 7.804 s. Rendering, not game update, dominates. The repeated fractional-parallax GL path queries texture allocation, NPOT support and four sampler parameters every draw/frame; target its invariant metadata before touching appearance.

## Implementation gate
- `--debug` now enables the wall-clock FPS HUD; `--profile` also implies it, while legacy `--show-fps` remains. Overlay moved to the final UI layer so gameplay modals/loading fades cannot cover it.
- Renderer optimization caches allocation dimensions and NPOT capability for each live fractional-parallax SDL texture/GL object, retaining per-draw sampler-state query/restore for visual correctness. This removes repeated synchronous texture-level/extension queries from the measured hot path. Building and measuring before/after with the same debug/profile settings next.

## First A/B checkpoint
- Matched debug-FPS baseline (`--profile --show-fps`) on NVE7: p50 21.987 ms, p95 23.789 ms; optimized metadata-cache trial (`--profile`, which now implies FPS): p50 21.440 ms, p95 37.000 ms. Endpoints and resolution match; both have 31,461 sprite calls and visible FPS pixels. The p95 regression amid shared GPU traffic means this alone is **not accepted** as a measured substantive gain. Continuing with sampler-query hot-path reduction and serial repeat trials.

## Render-target experiment
- Repeated same-route sampler-cache run measured p50 21.862 ms / p95 23.798 ms, essentially baseline 21.987/23.789 ms. Repeated GL metadata/sampler queries were not the material bottleneck. Rendering remains dominant: 4.285 s in sprite copies, 0.370 s present, with substantial additional GPU postprocess/other draw time. Next controlled experiment renders scene to a GPU target texture and samples it directly in Sepia.fx, eliminating the framebuffer-to-texture copy while preserving the HUD-after-sepia order. This is not accepted until physical-GPU A/B and visual check.

## Final physical-GPU verification gate
- Final strict accelerated build `port/build/performance-final/deathleak-linux`; live `x11` + `opengl` + `accelerated=1 software=0 vsync=1`, `vendor=nouveau renderer=NVE7`; 1280x720, same Forest-1 `B:360` / fixed-dt / FPS-on / 120-frame warmup / 240 sampled frames as baseline. Endpoint identical `(2808.99,3118.11)`, 7 orbs, 25 health; 31,461 sprite calls in both runs.
- Matched baseline `baseline-fps.log`: p50 21.9874 ms, p95 23.7888 ms, copy 4.29235 s, draw/present 8.29641 s. Final `final.log`: p50 20.9764 ms, p95 22.8177 ms, copy 1.33259 s, draw/present 7.98178 s. End-to-end median/p95 improve 4.6%/4.1%; sprite-copy measured bucket improves 69%. Shared-GPU runs did show tail variance (a separate trial p95 38 ms), so this is a modest end-to-end gain, not a claim of locked 60 FPS.
- Original render-target experiment was vertically inverted; corrected UV orientation verified against same-route captured frame: downsampled gameplay interior mean absolute RGB difference 3.13 upright versus 15.61 if flipped. This is a sanity check, not exact pixel-parity proof. Final `--debug` and default captures on physical NVE7 showed 292 versus 0 bright FPS-glyph pixels in bottom-left region, with active Forest gameplay; `--profile` implies the same overlay. Build passes; `git diff --check` passes. No release-complete claim.
- Renderer source had unrelated preexisting raft-mip changes; only own render-target/parallax-cache hunks were staged, leaving raft experiment unstaged. Evidence logs and BMPs under ignored `port/build/performance-*`.

## Exact-staged-source verification
- Built the staged index version of `SDLRenderer.cpp` mounted over working-tree renderer, excluding the unrelated unstaged raft experiment, then ran the same 360-frame physical-GPU route. `port/build/performance-staged/staged.log`: p50 20.9234 ms / p95 22.7963 ms, 31,461 sprite calls, copy 0.959246 s, identical endpoint. This confirms the scoped commit builds and retains the measured ~4.8% p50 / ~4.2% p95 improvement relative to matched FPS-on baseline.

## Commit and push gate
- Scoped commits `a7f836d` (debug FPS and measured checkpoint) and `2970985` (GPU scene render target) were pushed to `origin/feature/linux-renderer-foundation` without force. Only staged owned renderer hunks were committed; preexisting raft-mip renderer diff and all other dirty work remain unstaged. This status closure is a separate documentation commit.

## Follow-up GPU investigation and incremental text batch — 2026-10-23
- Physical nouveau NVE7 / Xorg :0, 1280x720, accelerated SDL OpenGL with vsync, same Forest-1 active `B:360` route, fixed simulation dt, warmup 120 / samples 240 and FPS enabled. A temporary diagnostic build measured the warmed 239-frame phases: scene submission 0.494 s, postprocess 0.013 s, post-sepia HUD 3.313 s, present 1.159 s. These are CPU-side phase durations; deferred GPU work can move to present.
- Bitmap text was a bounded hot path: every lit pixel previously called `FillRect`. `Renderer::FillRects` now provides a backwards-compatible default loop, and SDL submits a same-color label as one `SDL_RenderFillRectsF` batch after applying the same projection. Label order and individual rectangle coordinates are unchanged.
- Strict physical-GPU serial A/B with pre-change diagnostic binary and candidate: before p50/p95 20.9694/22.8091 and 20.9986/22.8201 ms; after 20.1472/21.9242 and 20.1522/21.9912 ms. This is ~3.9–4.1% p50 and ~3.6–3.9% p95, an incremental improvement only. Endpoint remained `(2808.99,3118.11)`, 7 orbs, 25 health, 22 shots fired. First-frame GPU BMPs with FPS enabled were byte-for-byte pixel-identical (1280x720, difference bbox none).
- Diagnostic ablations are **not** shipped: omitting gameplay film reached 19.159 ms p50 but removes authored visuals; hiding Forest XML IDs 37/21/10/15/22/17+19 yielded 19.89/19.77/19.77/20.01/19.00/19.06 ms p50 respectively but changes the scene. Replacing ordinary repeated sprites with the existing private-GL single-quad path measured 20.113 ms p50 versus the text-batch candidate 20.131 ms and was discarded. No isolated large render cost was found that can be removed while preserving visual parity.
- This follow-up does **not** meet the requested substantial optimization or 60-FPS target on NVE7. Further work requires a distinct GPU-side rendering redesign or a different verified hotspot; no release-complete claim. Evidence logs/BMPs in ignored `port/build/performance-next/`.
- Exact-staged-source build excluding pre-existing raft-mip renderer edits passed (binary SHA-256 `640d6f966a2068a239d40418598ac24aa4075951a2e9b1a3f7f85c0723bcdd29`). Physical NVE7 staged run: p50 20.1383 / p95 22.0002 ms with the same gameplay endpoint. Explicit `--debug` versus default first-frame captures differ only in the FPS glyph region `(10,693)-(80,707)`, 216 changed pixels. `git diff --cached --check` passed.

## Root-cause continuation — 2026-10-23 (in progress)
- Re-read exact current HUD path and prior phase evidence. The 3.313 s/239-frame post-sepia bucket includes fullscreen authored film, two bars, orb/meter sprites, batched bitmap text, optional FPS overlay, and any deferred GL/SDL synchronization; it cannot be labeled CPU font work from the phase timer alone. Film frames are lazy-loaded once (`filmFrames[0]` guard), and bitmap labels use stack/vector rectangle generation plus batched SDL fill; no per-frame font texture creation or readback is apparent. A readback occurs only for requested capture/packet frames.
- Building an isolated diagnostic binary with environment-selected no-film/no-bars/no-HUD/no-FPS/no-UI ablations. Source lives temporarily under `port/linux/.perf_diag_main_5880150d.cpp`; no tracked game file edited. Next: serial physical NVE7 A/B on the same active Forest route and warmup, with software renderer forbidden; interpret end-to-end deltas, not CPU phase attribution alone.
- Serial strict NVE7 1280×720, 360-frame active Forest `B:360`, fixed 1/60 simulation, warmup 120/FPS-on ablations finished. Diagnostic-only no-work controls: baseline repeats p50/p95 20.0968/21.9858 and 20.1781/22.0149 ms; no FPS glyph 20.1226/21.9968; no HUD widget 20.0924/21.8661; no top/bottom bars 20.0857/21.9309; no full-screen film 19.1298/21.0182; no UI 19.0140/20.8207. The actual FPS glyph contributes ~0 within noise; meter/orb/font are not the 13.9-ms/frame root cause. Film is ~1 ms/frame of real cost, but omitting it breaks authored visuals. This identifies deferred GPU/driver/composition time being charged to the post-sepia bucket, not per-frame font texture uploads. No ablation is shippable.
- Remaining no-UI scene still ~19 ms/frame, so the feasible 60-Hz headroom at current frame complexity is small. Next bounded discriminator: strict-NVE7 trivial-frame / scene-skip baseline and on/off-vsync timing; then decide whether a visual-preserving scene/postprocess redesign is justified. Logs under ignored `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/`.
- Strict physical GPU scene/trivial controls (new isolated binary) confirm a vsync/compositor floor: no scene but full UI/postprocess p50 16.643 ms, trivial no-scene/no-post/no-UI p50 16.645 ms, both `x11/opengl accelerated=1 software=0 vsync=1 nouveau/NVE7`. The matched full-scene controls in this second run were much slower (p50 34.436 then 41.361 ms), indicating contemporaneous shared-GPU interference; do not subtract these from the earlier serial HUD set or claim a fixed hardware ceiling. The no-scene/trivial controls do establish that the prior 13.9-ms CPU-labeled HUD phase is not intrinsic font/widget CPU work and that even a nearly empty frame is paced at ~16.6 ms by swap/vsync.
- Root-cause disposition: no per-frame film texture creation after initial load; no regular-frame readback; optional capture/packet only. FPS drawing itself is below run noise. Fullscreen film contributes ~1 ms end-to-end, while the retained scene/postprocess exceeds the 16.6-ms period by a few ms under the least-contended full-scene runs. Removing film or scene layers changes authored visuals, and previous pixel-preserving batch/render-target fixes have already been shipped. There is no evidenced additional **substantial** visual-preserving optimization from these ablations, so no speculative renderer software change is shipped here.
- Bounded next experiment: reserve an otherwise idle NVE7 display/GPU slot, collect `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` query pairs around (1) SDL scene queue flush, (2) sepia full-screen pass, (3) UI queue flush and (4) present separately, with query results read one or more frames later; interleave full gameplay, no-film and trivial controls in repeated ABBA order while recording Xorg/compositor GPU users and vsync state. If the scene shader/overdraw is dominant, prototype a same-pixel render-pass fusion or cached scene layer only after validating GPU time and exact-frame image parity. Stop at a measured pass-level bottleneck, not a CPU timestamp label.

## GL timer-query continuation — 2026-10-23 (in progress)
- Current worktree still has unrelated dirty `SDLRenderer.cpp`, integration/status files, and extensive untracked worker reports; performance instrumentation will be built from a temporary renderer copy and will not stage these.
- Physical Xorg `:0` is present; preflight process list showed Xorg only, no concurrent Death Leak/FFmpeg/capture process. Preparing nonblocking delayed `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` measurements around scene target submission, sepia pass, and post-sepia HUD submission before swap, plus serial matched active gameplay/trivial/no-film controls.
- Initial delayed GL timer runs on strict accelerated x11/opengl nouveau/NVE7 succeeded (`GL_ARB_timer_query`). Idle-GPU full gameplay repeats (`base1`, `clean_base3`) have frame p50 22.115/21.954 ms; game update 0.165 s/360 frames (~0.46 ms/frame); CPU scene-build/submit p50 3.66/3.57 ms, sepia CPU ~0.09 ms, HUD submit ~0.5 ms, while CPU present/swap p50 19.0/18.7 ms. GPU query scene p50 20.0/25.4 ms, sepia ~0.9/1.47, UI ~1.24/1.86; query values are **not yet pass-work attribution** because of visible scheduling/pacing and run variance.
- A clean no-scene but full postprocess+UI control has frame p50 16.664 ms and CPU scene submission p50 0.081 ms. Its CPU sepia call p50 15.606 ms, present p50 0.096 ms, and GL scene query p50 13.37 ms despite only target clear. Therefore vsync/driver pacing moves between calls, and GL_TIME_ELAPSED includes a large queue/schedule floor. Competing capture invalidated `base2` (p50 40.071 ms, GL scene p50 37.71 ms); exclude it. A no-film control reduced GL UI p50 to ~0.62 ms from full ~1.24–1.86 but end-to-end varied; no visual-preserving fix indicated.
- Building corrected query-frame accounting plus a diagnostic `SDL_GL_SetSwapInterval(0)` control to separate work from presentation cadence. No shipped source changes yet.
- Final swap-interval discriminator (`SDL_GL_SetSwapInterval(0)` returned 0, `SDL_GL_GetSwapInterval()==0`; SDL remains accelerated OpenGL nouveau/NVE7): no-scene controls p50 **15.957/15.961 ms**, GL scene query p50 **0.450/0.449 ms**; full Forest controls p50 **21.586/21.389 ms**, GL scene query p50 **19.449/19.261 ms**, sepia p50 ~0.90 ms, UI p50 ~1.23 ms. A third full control had p50 29.025 ms and scene query 25.703 ms despite zero competing capture processes in its pre/post snapshots; retained as GPU/power-state variance, not averaged away. All six controls ended at the same active-gameplay state `(2808.99,3118.11)`, 7 orbs, 25 health, 22 fired; all pre/post process snapshots showed zero competing Death Leak capture, FFmpeg, MPV, or GL demo. Source, script, logs, and snapshots are under ignored `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/`.
- Interpretation: update work is ~0.5 ms/frame and CPU scene submission ~2–4 ms; CPU is not the 50-FPS bottleneck. Full-scene GPU work dominates (roughly 19 ms query median at swap interval 0 versus 0.45 ms clear-only), with ~2 ms of sepia+UI and a ~16-ms no-scene wall-clock present/compositor/driver floor. With vsync on, no-scene CPU wait moves into `ApplyScenePostprocess` (~15.6 ms) and full-scene wait moves into `SDL_RenderPresent` (~18.7–19 ms); phase-labeled CPU wall times are synchronization, not CPU font work. Even swap interval 0 leaves `SDL_RenderPresent` blocking ~15 ms on the no-scene control, so the SDL/Xorg/driver path still has a presentation floor. GL_TIME_ELAPSED at vsync=1 likewise includes scheduling; the swap-off, clear-only control is required to interpret it.
- No visual-preserving software fix was identified. Omitting film reduces UI GPU query from ~1.23 to ~0.26 ms on a swap-off control, but deletes authored visual content; its full-frame timing also varied with scene GPU speed and is not accepted as a shippable gain. Other prior layer ablations similarly alter the picture. No tracked renderer/game code was changed in this continuation. The next bounded experiment is GPU subpass queries around authored scene painter bands/large fullscreen sprites **with GPU clocks and process-lane telemetry**, then a same-pixel optimization prototype of whichever band is actually dominant; require matched lossless gameplay frames and repeated A/B before shipping. Current evidence supports GPU scene bottleneck but not a safe specific pixel-preserving rewrite. Temporary diagnostic sources were moved into the ignored evidence packet, leaving unrelated dirty renderer/tests untouched.

## Physical-GPU scene subpass continuation — 2026-10-23/24
- Built a temporary, untracked SDL/OpenGL diagnostic from the current renderer and `LevelScene::Draw`, with delayed `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` queries split at painter depth boundaries. Runs used strict accelerated Xorg `:0` / SDL OpenGL / nouveau NVE7 at 1280×720, requested and confirmed swap interval 0, active Forest-1 `B:360`, fixed `1/60` simulation dt, `--profile` (FPS HUD enabled), 120 warmup and 240 measured frames. Query summaries were corrected to select their original frame IDs, not their delayed read frame IDs. Diagnostic code/logs/process snapshots are retained in ignored `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/`; no tracked renderer/game source was changed.
- Corrected depth-band controls `depth-final-a` / `depth-final-b`: frame p50/p95 **20.1363/22.0330** and **20.1791/22.0488 ms**; GPU-query p50, respectively: target clear before painter **0.447/0.447 ms**, depths >5000 (far/middle background) **8.803/7.222 ms**, depths 3001–5000 **4.325/6.090 ms**, depths 1001–3000 **3.349/3.350 ms**, depths <=1000 **0.993/0.999 ms** (240 warmed samples each). The far/middle background is the largest measured contiguous scene interval in both controls, but time moves between it and the following band while wall-frame time remains stable. Query intervals can include driver scheduling, so these are measured interval times, not additive intrinsic shader costs.
- The >5000 band contains XML IDs 1, 19, 20, 21, and 38. More granular queries localized visible work around the fractional-parallax tree strip ID 1 and repeated dark tree strip ID 21, but per-ID query medians varied between runs even with stable whole-frame timing (ID 1 ~2.5–4.8 ms; ID 21 ~2.1–2.6 ms). Do **not** claim these as precise independent GPU costs. Matched diagnostic hide controls give the defensible marginal bound: full controls p50 **20.244/20.161/20.105 ms**; omit ID 1 p50 **18.623/18.575 ms**; omit ID 21 p50 **19.848/19.793 ms**. Endpoints were identical `(2808.99,3118.11)`, 7 orbs, 25 health, 22 shots fired. Omitting ID 1 saves ~1.5–1.6 ms and ID 21 ~0.3–0.4 ms, but both erase authored imagery and are diagnostic only.
- Process snapshots around each run showed Xorg as the only other visible display/GPU process (no competing Death Leak, FFmpeg, MPV, GL demo, or compiler); PCI power state was D0. This NVE7 host exposes no usable GPU-frequency counter through the available DRM sysfs/hwmon/debugfs paths, so clock stability is unverified and per-sprite query scheduling variance is retained as a limitation.
- **Disposition:** no visual-preserving optimization was shipped. The largest individually removable visual layer saves less than half the ~3.5-ms median gap from the current ~20.1-ms frame to the 16.667-ms 60-Hz budget, and prior ordinary-sprite private-GL substitution was neutral. A safe next prototype must target multiple backplane overdraw passes, e.g. a same-pixel static/co-moving background tile cache or compositing fusion that preserves each layer's independent camera/UV phase and alpha order. Gate it on exact lossless pixel parity at multiple camera positions and film frames, repeated serial NVE7 A/B frame timing and unchanged gameplay endpoint; reject it if an extra resampling step changes pixels or gain stays within lane variance. Merely deleting ID 1/21, lowering render resolution, or interpreting a query interval as an isolated shader cost is not an acceptable fix. The existing FPS/debug feature and prior staged-source pixel check remain unchanged; this continuation had a successful diagnostic build and real-GPU run but no new software release claim.

## Same-pixel backplane-cache prototype — 2026-10-23/24
- Prototyped a route-local far/middle backplane cache **only in ignored diagnostic sources** under `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/` (`make_backplane_cache.py`, `backplane_renderer.cpp`, `backplane_LevelScene.cpp`, `run-backplane.sh`; build and four A/B logs/BMPs alongside). No tracked renderer/gameplay code was changed. The cache copies the completed >5000-depth scene band into a 1280×720 RGBA8888 SDL target after the original painter pass; on an exact camera-X/Y repeat, it copies that target back and skips the five far-band sprites. This is a deliberately conservative, testable cache attempt, not a release implementation.
- The state probe (`cache-state-a.log`, `cache_state_LevelScene.cpp`) found 80 consecutive exact ID-1 draw-command repeats within the 240 warmed Forest-1 frames, concentrated at the route's camera clamp; the other moving frames change fractional UV phase and/or projected positions. IDs 1/19/20/21/38 have independent placement/UV phases, so a fixed static atlas would not preserve their painter order throughout movement. The candidate registered 81 hits and 279 misses over all 360 frames in each trial.
- Serial strict physical NVE7/Xorg `:0`, accelerated SDL OpenGL (`software=0`), confirmed swap interval 0, 1280×720, same `B:360` active Forest route, fixed 1/60 dt, 120 warmup / 240 measured frames, FPS HUD on, idle display lane (pre-run snapshots show Xorg only). ABBA order: cache off A p50/p95 **20.2081/22.0541 ms**, cache on A **20.9945/22.8932**, cache on B **20.9709/22.8691**, cache off B **20.2330/22.0613**. Candidate is ~0.74–0.79 ms slower at the median and ~0.81–0.84 ms slower at p95. The far-band query median moves from ~7.23–7.25 ms off to ~6.72–6.77 on while pre-band clear/copy query grows from ~0.45 to ~3.63–3.65 ms; these query intervals include scheduling and should not be interpreted as additive shader cost. All four runs have the identical gameplay endpoint `(2808.99,3118.11)`, 7 orbs, 25 health, 22 shots fired.
- **Lossless parity failed.** The two cache-off 1280×720 captured BMPs decode to identical RGB pixels; cache-on A differs from off A in **64,833** RGB pixels (bbox x=0..1183, y=235..706), cache-on B differs from off B in **598,225** pixels, and the two cache-on captures differ in 584,324 pixels. This is not attributable solely to the small FPS glyph region; the candidate introduces a sampled target copy and/or target-state error, and the run-to-run variation has not been isolated. Both parity and substantial-gain gates fail, so the prototype is **rejected** and no renderer code is shipped.
- Next bounded experiment, if performance work continues: isolate the backplane target-copy discrepancy with pre-/post-copy **scene-target** readbacks at the first cache hit, matching framebuffer orientation, blend state, alpha, and exact viewport; only then try a same-format framebuffer blit (or direct painter-band fusion) and re-run lossless parity at moving and clamped camera frames. If parity is exact, measure a cache design that does not add a full-frame copy on every miss. Do not infer 60-FPS/release readiness from this route: real-display GPU acceptance remains outstanding.

## Target-copy isolation — 2026-10-23/24
- Continued the rejected backplane prototype using two additional **ignored diagnostic-only** variants in `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/`. Both ran the same 1280×720 active Forest `B:360` route, 120-frame warmup / 240 measured frames, fixed simulation dt, strict accelerated SDL OpenGL on physical nouveau/NVE7, and confirmed swap interval 0. No tracked renderer/gameplay source was changed.
- **No-copy control:** created the band target and ran the cache-state bookkeeping but skipped every target copy and cache hit. Against the prior cache-off A capture, only 56 RGB pixels differed, all within the live FPS glyph at `(70,693)–(80,707)`; the gameplay image was otherwise losslessly identical. Frame p50/p95 was 20.1126/21.9908 ms, consistent with the prior 20.2081/22.0541-ms cache-off control given lane variance.
- **Miss-only copy control:** forced zero hits (`hits=0`, `misses=360`) while retaining the scene-target-to-band-target copy after every far-band draw. This already changed **567,760** RGB pixels versus cache-off A (bbox `(0,33)–(1279,706)`; 2,278 pixels differed by >10 RGB levels). Frame p50/p95 was **21.0322/22.8953 ms**. The copy itself therefore causes a visual difference and about 0.8–0.9 ms/frame overhead in these matched conditions; stale hits are not required to reproduce either failure. This does not yet identify whether the cause is SDL target switching, source-texture sampling/format, alpha/state, or capture timing after the copy.
- The rejected cache-on A had 64,833 changed pixels, mostly <=10 RGB levels; cache-on B had 598,225 changed pixels. The large variation remains unexplained, but the no-copy control narrows the first causal boundary to the extra render-target copy. Do not ship this cache or treat its partial GPU-band query reduction as a net improvement. A copy-free design would need a different approach and still must pass lossless multi-frame parity and repeated physical-GPU A/B; 60 FPS remains unproven.

## 2026-10-25 bounded far-band alpha-cull discriminator
- Started from HEAD `d8e17b5`; the existing dirty renderer, tests, and reports were left untouched. Inspected the prior strict physical NVE7/Xorg `:0` scene-band A/B in `port/build/performance-root-5880150d/`: full Forest `B:360` controls p50 20.244/20.161/20.105 ms, omit XML ID 1 p50 18.623/18.575 ms, omit ID 21 p50 19.848/19.793 ms (swap interval 0, 120 warmup, 240 measured). Actual `glBindTexture` calls were **9,330 vs 8,850** (median 155.5 vs 147.5/frame), a reduction of exactly 480 / 60 = **8 binds/frame** in this window. Thus atlases reduce binds on this route but **not submissions**; SDL's draw queue has not become a multi-quad batch by sharing page identity. Full trace has 395 vs 385 `glTexImage2D` allocations and 27,080 vs 25,649 binds.
- All four atlas pages appear in opt-in GL allocation sizes (2146×315 icons, 212×124 HUD, 1710×576 branches, 1032×581 carts). The combined pages trade fewer texture objects for ~6.30 MB more allocated RGBA in this route. No true residency/eviction telemetry is available yet.

### DONE — physical-NVE7 per-fog GPU draw vs SDL flush split — 2026-10-25
- Frozen clean HEAD renderer (`2b0448d2...3b5ff`) and current `LevelScene.cpp` were instrumented only in ignored `port/build/performance-foggpu-20261025/`; no tracked game/renderer source edit. Isolated Docker build passed. Two serial 800-frame fixed-dt `R:800,L:1200` runs used physical Xorg `:0`, SDL `x11`/OpenGL accelerated=1/software=0, `nouveau/NVE7`, 1280x720; no competing Death Leak/apitrace/FFmpeg GPU process. Both entered Waterfall-top then bottom at the established gameplay endpoint `(633.985,1433.27)`, 11 orbs/25 health. Logs: `physical.log`, `physical-repeat.log` in that ignored directory.
- Delayed `GL_TIME_ELAPSED` queries bracketed **each** tagged fog private-GL quad `glBegin`→`glEnd`, and separately its preceding `SDL_RenderFlush`. Results were read in later frames. In steady Waterfall-top frames 450–740 (291 frames/run; 961 tagged draws/run), per-frame sum of fog **draw** query p50/p95/p99 was **3.842/4.269/4.298 ms** then **3.833/4.277/4.302 ms**. The corresponding `SDL_RenderFlush` query sum was **0.0039/0.0056/0.0082 ms** then **0.0039/0.0057/0.0089 ms**; CPU flush-call sum p50 was 0.0105/0.0101 ms. Draw p50 by ID (run A / B): 117 1.034/1.031 ms (291 calls each), 118 1.245/1.246 ms (291), 119 0.201/0.197 ms (88), 121 1.414/1.411 ms (291); ID120 had no tagged fog quad in this window. At ordinals 600/638/700 the IDs were 117/118/121, 117/118/121, and 117/118/119/121 respectively, matching prior ordered packet counts.
- **Mechanism:** the expensive measured slot is the submitted translucent PixelWobble fog quads, not the explicit SDL flush call or CPU GL-state restoration. `glBegin`→`glEnd` timing includes raster/blend/texture/shader execution and may include driver scheduling; it does not independently prove which fragment operation dominates. The flush query excludes GPU work already enqueued before its begin marker, so this is specifically *flush-call interval*, not total preceding SDL scene work. The prior ~3.161-ms combined fog interval and this ~3.84-ms per-frame draw sum differ in instrumentation/window and are not additive. Instrumented whole-route p50 ~21.03/21.08 ms is not an optimization A/B.
- **Candidate gate:** these 3–4 broad, semitransparent draws have different alpha/transforms and nonopaque content. A naive common-uniform batch, opaque-cover clip, fog omission, or dropping shader operations would change pixels; there is no measured parity-safe candidate yet. Even deleting all measured fog draw time would not by itself close Waterfall-top's roughly 14-ms gap to 16.67 ms. No production patch, exact-pixel candidate A/B, commit/push, FPS/8K/high-Hz/release acceptance is claimed. All unrelated dirty paths, including unstaged raft-mip renderer edits, remain intact; GPU/source ownership released.
- **Next distinct bounded task:** obtain a per-draw on-screen fragment/overdraw and shader-operation discriminator for IDs117/118/121 on NVE7 (e.g. conservative screen-space clipped quad vs original as *diagnostic* first, or a shader-cost ablation), then design a geometry/sampling reduction only if exact moving-camera RGBA parity is feasible.
